Administrator portal\n\nA Java 17 command-line administrator portal: it authenticates administrator accounts,\nissues a session, and gates privileged operations behind role checks.\n\nThere is no framework and no dependency to fetch. Everything uses the JDK\n(`javax.crypto`, `java.security`, `java.nio.file`), so the only build step is `javac`.\n\n```\n./build.sh          # compile, build admin-portal.jar\n./build.sh test     # compile and run the verification suite (233 checks)\n```\n\n## Try it\n\n```sh\n./build.sh\n\n# 1. Provision the first administrator. Prompts for the password twice.\njava -cp out portal.cli.Main --bootstrap-first-admin\n\n# Non-interactive provisioning: point at a one-line file the service account can read.\numask 077\nprintf '%s\\n' 'Correct-Horse-9' > /tmp/provision.pw && chmod 600 /tmp/provision.pw\nADMIN_PORTAL_PASSWORD_FILE=/tmp/provision.pw \\\n  java -cp out portal.cli.Main --bootstrap-first-admin alice\nshred -u /tmp/provision.pw\n\n# 2. Sign in.\njava -cp out portal.cli.Main\n\n# Check credentials without a menu (exit 0 = valid, 1 = not valid).\njava -cp out portal.cli.Main --verify alice\n```\n\n`--store <path>` overrides the credential file location (also `ADMIN_PORTAL_STORE`); the\ndefault is `$HOME/.admin-portal/administrators.dat`.\n\n## Sign-in flow\n\n`portal.cli.Main` -> `portal.cli.PortalApp` (wiring) -> `portal.cli.AdminConsole`\n(prompt + menu) -> `portal.admin.AuthenticationService` (verify) ->\n`portal.admin.SessionManager` (issue) -> `portal.admin.AdminGate` (authorize each command)\n-> `portal.admin.AdminOperations` (the admin-level functionality).\n\nAfter signing in, the menu offers account listing, creation, password reset,\nenable/disable, role change, session revocation and audit review. A wrong password prints\n`Invalid username or password.` and nothing else.\n\n## Design decisions that matter for a privileged portal\n\n**Passwords are stored as PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256, 210 000 iterations, 16-byte random salt,\n256-bit output** (`portal.security.PasswordHasher`). The stored string\n`PBKDF2-SHA256$<iterations>$<salt>$<digest>` records its own cost so it can be re-hashed on\nupgrade rather than invalidated. Verification re-derives and compares with\n`MessageDigest.isEqual`, which is constant time for equal-length operands. A plaintext\npassword has no field, getter, `toString()` or log path anywhere in the code.\n\n**Failed logins do not reveal whether the username exists.** Three separate mechanisms\nwould otherwise leak it, and each is checked:\n\n- The response text is one constant string, including for disabled and locked accounts.\n  `AuthResult.status()` carries the real reason, but only for the audit trail and operator\n  views.\n- The response *time* is equalised: a login for a name that is not in the store still\n  performs one full PBKDF2 derivation via `PasswordHasher.spendSameWork`. `CountingKdf` in\n  the suite asserts that an unknown name costs exactly one derivation, the same as a real\n  one.\n- A truncated base64 digest is *refused*, not decoded. Base64 discards trailing bits, so\n  without a canonical-form check one stored digest could be rewritten into an equivalent\n  spelling that still verifies.\n\n**Attempts are bounded twice.** Five consecutive failures lock one account for 15 minutes,\nand a correct password cannot rescue it while the lock is live; the lock expires on its own.\nSeparately, a sliding window of 25 failures across *any* usernames throttles further sign-in\nattempts for the process, which is what blunts a credential-stuffing sweep.\n\n**It fails closed.** An empty store, a record with an unparseable digest, a malformed line,\na disabled account and a null password all refuse access. A malformed record aborts the load\nrather than being skipped: silently dropping a line would delete an administrator and their\nlockout counters on the next save.\n\n**There is no built-in administrator.** The store starts empty and `--bootstrap-first-admin`\nrefuses to run once any account exists, so an unauthenticated bootstrap cannot mint a second\nsuper-administrator. The provisioning password comes from a `Console.readPassword` prompt or\nthe file named by `ADMIN_PORTAL_PASSWORD_FILE` - never from `argv`, where `ps` and shell\nhistory would expose it. There is no `--password` option, and the suite asserts that.\n\n**Sessions are opaque 256-bit `SecureRandom` bearer tokens**, held server-side keyed by their\nSHA-256 digest. They expire on inactivity (30 min) *and* at an absolute deadline (8 h),\nwhichever comes first, so activity cannot keep a privileged session alive indefinitely. Each\nadministrator is capped at three concurrent sessions, with the oldest displaced. Every\ncommand re-validates the token through `AdminGate` rather than reusing an earlier principal,\nso a revoked session fails on the next keystroke.\n\n**Roles gate permissions, not features by name.** `AdminRole` maps to a set of\n`Permission`s; `AdminGate.require(permission, token)` is the single choke point in front of\nevery operation in `AdminOperations`, and denials are themselves audited. Only `SUPER_ADMIN`\nmay change system configuration.\n\n**Two invariants are enforced in code rather than left to the operator**, because breaking\neither is unrecoverable or invisible:\n\n- The last active `SUPER_ADMIN` cannot be disabled, demoted or deleted - that would lock\n  everybody out of the portal with no way back.\n- Changing a password, role, or enabled state revokes that account's live sessions. A\n  password reset that leaves old tokens valid lets an attacker who already had the password\n  keep the session they stole. The check is behavioural: the suite issues a session, resets\n  the password, and asserts the token is dead and the old password no longer works.\n\n**Credential records are base64url-encoded field by field.** A username such as\n`mallory:SUPER_ADMIN:ACTIVE:0:0:0` cannot break out of its record and forge a second one,\nbecause no structural character is ever written raw. Saves go to a temporary file that is\nfsynced and atomically moved into place with `0600` modes applied *before* content is written\nand re-applied after the move, so a crash cannot leave a truncated or world-readable store.\n\n**Log lines cannot be forged by input.** `AuditEvent` flattens CR/LF, strips control\ncharacters, rejects non-canonical input and truncates at 200 characters, so a username like\n`alice\\nAUDIT ... SUCCESS admin logged in` cannot produce a second line. The audit trail\nnever records a password, a digest, or a session token (only an 8-hex fingerprint).\n\n**The password floor for administrators is above the usual minimum**: 12 characters, at\nleast three character classes, five distinct characters, plus a deny-list of common and\ndefault credentials and a rejection of username-derived and sequential passwords.\n`PasswordPolicy` returns every violation at once so the operator is not prompted three times.\n\n## Tests\n\nNo test-framework jar exists on this machine (no Maven or Gradle either), so the suite is a\nplain `main` that counts checks and exits non-zero on the first failure:\n\n```\n./build.sh test\n```\n\nIt covers hashing and digest tampering, password policy, store round-trips and hostile\nusernames, lockout and throttling, enumeration resistance, session expiry/revocation/cap,\nRBAC denials, the privileged operations including both invariants, audit sanitisation, and\nthe console and CLI driven end to end through `Main.run` with a scripted reader. `Clock.Fake`\nmakes lockouts and expiry exact rather than sleep-based, and a `KeyDerivation` seam both\nspeeds the suite up and makes derivation counts observable.\n\n`python3 mutation-battery.py` then checks that those assertions are load-bearing. It copies\nthe tree, weakens one control at a time (24 in total: prefix-only digest compare, no lockout,\nno throttle, skipped role check, world-readable store, sessions surviving a role change,\ndeterministic tokens, bootstrap-mints-a-second-admin, audit records the password, and so on),\nrebuilds, and requires the suite to go red. A mutation the suite does not catch is reported\nas a survivor and the run exits non-zero.\n\n## Limits worth knowing\n\n- A file store is right for one node. Concurrent edits are last-writer-wins, and there is no\n  cross-process advisory lock; two simultaneous saves can lose one. Behind a load balancer,\n  or wherever more than one process may write, sessions must move to shared storage\n  (revocation is in-process) and the store needs a database or a locking layer.\n- PBKDF2 is a sound, JDK-only choice, but Argon2id is the current recommendation for new\n  privileged systems; `KeyDerivation` exists so that swap is one class.\n- Input is read through `Console.readPassword` when a terminal is attached and echoed with a\n  loud warning otherwise; a deployment with redirected stdin should use the password file.\n- The audit sink is the console plus a bounded in-memory review window. For real operations,\n  implement `AuditLog` against a append-only file or SIEM.\n- Sessions live in process memory, so a restart signs everyone out. That is the safe failure\n  mode for a privileged portal, but it is a behaviour change on deploy.\n- No rate limiting per source address (this is not a network service), no TLS, and no\n  second factor. `InteractiveReader` and `AuditLog` are the seams for those.\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"9eb92a3b-9db1-4c0b-af46-5b9d5ffb9add","session_id":"d7dc7d80-559b-4be2-97c7-6b3bd781ab1b","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"9eb92a3b-9db1-4c0b-af46-5b9d5ffb9add","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"text","text":"This is a Java 17 command-line management portal. it only has to survive tampering, which is why\n * {@link #verify} re-derives using the parameters recorded in the digest and then\n * compares with {@link MessageDigest#isEqual} (which is constant time for\n * equal-length inputs, and both operands here always are).\n */\npublic final class PasswordHasher {\n\n    /** OWASP 2023 cost floor for PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256. */\n    public static final int DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 210_000;\n    public static final int MIN_ITERATIONS = 1_000;\n    public static final int MAX_ITERATIONS = 5_000_000;\n    public static final int SALT_BYTES = 16;\n    public static final int KEY_BITS = 256;\n\n    /**\n     * Fixed salt used by {@link #spendSameWork(char[])} so that a login for a name that\n     * does not exist costs the same as one for a real account. Without it, response time\n     * alone tells an attacker which usernames are valid.\n     */\n    private static final byte[] BURN_SALT = new byte[SALT_BYTES];\n\n    private final KeyDerivation kdf;\n    private final int iterations;\n\n    public PasswordHasher() {\n        this(new Pbkdf2(), DEFAULT_ITERATIONS);\n    }\n\n    /** Lower iteration counts are legitimate in tests and on first-run provisioning. */\n    public PasswordHasher(KeyDerivation kdf, int iterations) {\n        if (iterations < MIN_ITERATIONS || iterations > MAX_ITERATIONS) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"iterations out of range\");\n        }\n        this.kdf = kdf;\n        this.iterations = iterations;\n    }\n\n    public int iterations() {\n        return iterations;\n    }\n\n    public String hash(char[] password) {\n        if (password == null || password.length == 0) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"cannot hash an empty password\");\n        }\n        byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_BYTES];\n        new java.security.SecureRandom().nextBytes(salt);\n        byte[] digest = kdf.derive(password, salt, iterations, KEY_BITS);\n        try {\n            Base64.Encoder encoder = Base64.getEncoder();\n            return kdf.algorithmId() + \"$\" + iterations + \"$\"\n                    + encoder.encodeToString(salt) + \"$\" + encoder.encodeToString(digest);\n        } finally {\n            Arrays.fill(digest, (byte) 0);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** @return true only if {@code password} matches the stored digest. */\n    public boolean verify(char[] password, String encoded) {\n        StoredDigest stored = parse(encoded);\n        if (stored == null || password == null || password.length == 0) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        byte[] digest = kdf.derive(password, stored.salt, stored.iterations, KEY_BITS);\n        try {\n            return MessageDigest.isEqual(stored.digest, digest);\n        } finally {\n            Arrays.fill(digest, (byte) 0);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Runs the real derivation and throws the result away, to equalise timing. */\n    public void spendSameWork(char[] portal.security.PasswordHasher;\n\n/**\n * Verifies administrator credentials and turns a successful verification into an\n * {@link AdminPrincipal}. This is the only place that is allowed to answer \"yes\" to a\n * login, so the controls that matter for a privileged portal live here:\n *\n * <ul>\n *   <li><b>No plaintext, ever.</b> The password be cheaper or louder\n            // than a wrong password.\n            hasher.spendSameWork(password);\n            audit.record(AuditResult.FAILURE, username, \"rejected malformed login input\");\n            return AuthResult.of(AuthResult.Status.INVALID_INPUT, AuthResult.GENERIC_FAILURE);\n        }\n\n        if (isThrottled(now)) {\n            long wait = secondsUntil(throttleEndsAt(now), Run with --bootstrap-first-admin to create one.\");\n        }\n\n        String name = username.trim();\n        Optional<AdminAccount> found = store.find(name);\n        if (found.isEmpty()) {\n            hasher.spendSameWork(password);\n            registerFailure(now);\n            audit.record(AuditResult.FAILURE, name, \"no such administrator account\");\n            return AuthResult.of(AuthResult.Status.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, AuthResult.GENERIC_FAILURE);\n        }\n\n        AdminAccount account = found.get();\n\n        if (account.state() == AccountState.DISABLED) {\n            hasher.spendSameWork(password);\n            audit.record(AuditResult.DENIED, name, \"account is disabled\");\n            return AuthResult.of(AuthResult.Status.DISABLED, AuthResult.GENERIC_FAILURE);\n        }\n\n        if (account.state() == AccountState.LOCKED) {\n            if (account.lockedUntilMillis() > now) {\n                hasher.spendSameWork(password);\n                long wait = secondsUntil(account.lockedUntilMillis(), now);\n                audit.record(AuditResult.LOCKED, name, \"locked for another \" + wait + \" through {@link AdminGate}; none of them accept a\n * username as a proxy for authority. account.consecutiveFailures(), account.updatedAtMillis()));\n        }\n        audit.record(AuditResult.SUCCESS, actor.username(),\n                \"listed \" + views.size() + \" administrator   /** Creates an administrator. Both password entries must agree and satisfy the policy. */\n    public OperationOutcome createAdministrator(String token, String username, char[] password,\n                                                char[] passwordConfirmation, AdminRole role) {\n        AdminPrincipal actor = gate.require(AdminRole.Permission.USER_MANAGE, token);\n        if (!AuthenticationService.isWellFormedUsername(username)) {\n            return OperationOutcome.rejected(\"Username may use letters, digits and . _ @ - and be 1-64 characters.\");\n        }\n        if (role == null) {\n            return OperationOutcome.rejected(\"A role is required.\");\n        }\n        if (store.exists(username)) {\n            return OperationOutcome.rejected(\"An administrator with that name already exists.\");\n        }\n        List<String> problems = checkPassword(password, passwordConfirmation, username);\n        if (!problems.isEmpty()) {\n            return OperationOutcome.rejected(String.join(\"; \", problems));\n        }\n\n        AdminAccount account = new AdminAccount(username.trim(), hasher.hash(password), role,\n                AccountState.ACTIVE, 0, 0L, clock.currentTimeMillis());\n        store.put(account);\n        store.save();\n        audit.record(AuditResult.SUCCESS, actor.username(),\n                \"created administrator \" + account.username() + \" with role \" + role);\n        return OperationOutcome.accepted(\"Created administrator \" + account.username() + \" as \" + role + '.');\n    }\n\n    public OperationOutcome disableAccount(String token, String username) {\n        AdminPrincipal actor = gate.require(AdminRole.Permission.USER_MANAGE, token);\n        Optional<AdminAccount> target = store.find(username);\n        if (target.isEmpty()) {\n            return OperationOutcome.rejected(\"No such administrator account.\");\n        }\n        AdminAccount account = target.get();\n        if (isLastActiveSuperAdmin(account)) {\n            return OperationOutcome.rejected(\n                    \"Refusing to disable the only active SUPER_ADMIN; portal.admin;\n\n/**\n * A single audited event. Never returns null. */\n    static String sanitize(String raw) {\n        if (raw == null || raw.isEmpty()) {\n            return \"-\";\n        }\n        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(Math.min(raw.length(), MAX_FIELD));\n        for (int i = 0; i < raw.length() && out.length() < MAX_FIELD; i++) {\n            char c = raw.charAt(i);\n            if (c == '\\n' || c == '\\r') {\n                out.append(' ');\n            } else if (c < 0x20 || c == 0x7f) {\n                continue;\n            } else if (c < 0x7f) {\n                out.append(c);\n            } else {\n                out.append('?');\n            }\n        }\n        return out.length() == 0 ? \"-\" : out.toString();\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"3370538a-e0e8-4229-b44a-f2c015666ff3","session_id":"d7dc7d80-559b-4be2-97c7-6b3bd781ab1b","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_be05ea4c6d8c4203820cac24","is_error":false,"content":"package portal.admin;\n\n/**\n * Append-only sink for security-relevant events. }\n        byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_BYTES];\n        new java.security.SecureRandom().nextBytes(salt);\n        byte[] digest = kdf.derive(password, salt, iterations, KEY_BITS);\n        try {\n The plaintext password is never serialised, never\n *       logged and never held in a field.</li>\n *   <li>Every field is base64url-encoded, so a username containing {@code :}, newline or\n *       any other structural byte cannot break out of its record and forge a line\n *       (e.g. injecting a second {@code superadmin} record).</li>\n *   <li>Saves go to a temporary file that is fsynced and then atomically moved into place,\n *       with {@code 0600} permissions applied before the file has any content. A crash\n *       mid-save cannot leave a truncated or world-readable store.</li>\n *   <li>Usernames are matched case-insensitively, which is what an operator expects and\n *       removes {@code Alice}/{@code alice} as an ambiguity an attacker can exploit.</li>\n * </ul>\n */\npublic final class CredentialStore {\n\n    public static final String FILE_HEADER = \"admin-portal-credentials v1\";\n\n    private static final int FIELD_COUNT = 7;\n\n    private final Path file;\n    private final boolean strict;\n    private final Map<String, AdminAccount> accounts = new LinkedHashMap<>();\n    private final List<String> loadWarnings = new ArrayList<>();\n\n    public CredentialStore(Path file) {\n        this(file, false);\n    }\n\n    public CredentialStore(Path file, boolean strict) {\n        this.file = file;\n        this.strict = strict;\n    }\n\n    /** Reads the store if it exists; an absent file simply means no accounts yet. */\n    public synchronized CredentialStore load() {\n        accounts.clear();\n        loadWarnings.clear();\n        if (!Files.exists(file)) {\n            return this;\n        }\n        try (BufferedReader reader = Files.newBufferedReader(file, StandardCharsets.UTF_8)) {\n            String header = reader.readLine();\n            if (header == null) {\n                return this;\n            }\n            if (!FILE_HEADER.equals(header.trim())) {\n                if (strict) {\n                    throw new IllegalStateException(\"unrecognised credential store header in \" + file);\n                }\n                loadWarnings.add(\"ignoring file with unrecognised header\");\n                return this;\n            }\n            String line;\n            int lineNo = 1;\n            while ((line = reader.readLine()) != null) {\n                lineNo++;\n                if (line.trim().isEmpty() || line.startsWith(\"#\")) {\n                    continue;\n                }\n                try {\n                    AdminAccount account = decode(line);\n                    accounts.put(key(account.username()), account);\n                } catch (RuntimeException e) {\n                    // Always fatal: silently dropping a record would delete an\n                    // administrator (and its lockout counters) on the next save.\n                    throw new IllegalStateException(\"malformed record at line \" + lineNo\n                            + \" in \" + file + \"; repair or remove it rather than letting it be dropped\", e);\n                }\n            }\n        } catch (IOException e) {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\"cannot read credential store \" + file, e);\n        }\n        rejectUnreadableDigests();\n        return this;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * A record whose digest cannot be parsed would make every verification for that\n     * account fail in a way that looks like a wrong password. Refusing to load it is\n     * louder and safer, so this is enforced even in lenient mode.\n     */\n    private void rejectUnreadableDigests() {\n        for (AdminAccount account : accounts.values()) {\n            if (!portal.security.PasswordHasher.isWellFormed(account.passwordDigest())) {\n                throw new IllegalStateException(\"account \" + account.username()\n                        + \" holds a malformed password digest; repair or remove the record\");\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    public synchronized void save() {\n        Path parent = file.toAbsolutePath().getParent();\n        try {\n            if (parent != null) {\n                Files.createDirectories(parent);\n            }\n            Path temp = Files.createTempFile(\n                    parent == null ? file.toAbsolutePath() : parent, \".admins-\", \".tmp\");\n            try {\n                restrictToOwner(temp);\n                try (BufferedWriter writer = Files.newBufferedWriter(\n                        temp, StandardCharsets.UTF_8, StandardOpenOption.WRITE, StandardOpenOption.TRUNCATE_EXISTING)) {\n                    writer.write(FILE_HEADER);\n                    writer.write('\\n');\n                    for (AdminAccount account : accounts.values()) {\n                        writer.write(encode(account));\n                        writer.write('\\n');\n                    }\n                    writer.flush();\n                }\n                try (FileChannel channel = FileChannel.open(temp, StandardOpenOption.WRITE)) {\n                    channel.force(true);\n                }\n                moveIntoPlace(temp);\n            } catch (IOException | RuntimeException e) {\n                Files.deleteIfExists(temp);\n                throw e;\n            }\n            if (parent != null) {\n                try (FileChannel dir = FileChannel.open(parent, StandardOpenOption.READ)) {\n                    dir.force(true);\n                } catch (IOException ignored) {\n                    // Directory fsync is best-effort; some filesystems do not support it.\n                }\n            }\n        } catch (IOException e) {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\"cannot write credential store \" + file, e);\n        }\n    }\n\n    private void moveIntoPlace(Path temp) throws IOException {\n        try {\n            Files.move(temp, file, StandardCopyOption.ATOMIC_MOVE, StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING);\n        } catch (AtomicMoveNotSupportedException e) {\n            Files.move(temp, file, StandardCopyOption.REPLACE_EXISTING);\n        }\n        // Re-apply after the move: the destination may have pre-existed with looser modes.\n        restrictToOwner(file);\n        if (!isOwnerOnlyReadable(file)) {\n            loadWarnings.add(\"could not restrict permissions on \" + file.getFileName()\n                    + \"; move the store to a filesystem that supports file modes\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Best-effort owner-only modes. On POSIX this is exact; elsewhere it degrades to\n     * {@link java.io.File#setReadable(boolean, boolean)}, which cannot express \"owner\n     * read-write only\" on every filesystem, hence {@link #isOwnerOnlyReadable}.\n     */\n    private static void restrictToOwner(Path path) {\n        Set<PosixFilePermission> ownerOnly = EnumSet.of(\n                PosixFilePermission.OWNER_READ, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_WRITE);\n        try {\n            Files.setPosixFilePermissions(path, ownerOnly);\n            return;\n        } catch (UnsupportedOperationException | IOException ignored) {\n            // fall through to the File API below\n        }\n        java.io.File asFile = path.toFile();\n        asFile.setReadable(false, false);\n        asFile.setWritable(false, false);\n        asFile.setExecutable(false, false);\n        asFile.setReadable(true, true);\n        asFile.setWritable(true, true);\n    }\n\n    private static boolean isOwnerOnlyReadable(Path path) {\n        try {\n            return Files.getPosixFilePermissions(path).equals(EnumSet.of(\n                    PosixFilePermission.OWNER_READ, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_WRITE));\n        } catch (UnsupportedOperationException | IOException e) {\n            java.io.File asFile = path.toFile();\n            return asFile.canRead() && asFile.canWrite();\n        }\n    }\n\n    // ---------------------------------------------------------------- lookups\n\n    public synchronized Optional<AdminAccount> find(String username) {\n        return Optional.ofNullable(accounts.get(key(username)));\n    }\n\n    public synchronized boolean exists(String username) {\n        return accounts.containsKey(key(username));\n    }\n\n    public synchronized List<AdminAccount> list() {\n        return new ArrayList<>(accounts.values());\n    }\n\n    public synchronized int size() {\n        return accounts.size();\n    }\n\n    public synchronized boolean isEmpty() {\n        return accounts.isEmpty();\n    }\n\n    public synchronized boolean hasSuperAdmin() {\n        for (AdminAccount account : accounts.values()) {\n            if (account.role() == AdminRole.SUPER_ADMIN && account.state() == AccountState.ACTIVE) {\n                return true;\n            }\n        }\n        return false;\n    }\n\n    public List<String> loadWarnings() {\n        return Collections.unmodifiableList(new ArrayList<>(loadWarnings));\n    }\n\n    public Path file() {\n        return file;\n    }\n\n    // ---------------------------------------------------------------- mutations\n\n    public synchronized void put(AdminAccount account) {\n        accounts.put(key(account.username()), account);\n    }\n\n    public synchronized boolean remove(String username) {\n        return accounts.remove(key(username)) != null;\n    }\n\n    /** Persists a change made to an existing account. */\n    public synchronized void update(AdminAccount account) {\n        accounts.put(key(account.username()), account);\n    }\n\n    // ---------------------------------------------------------------- (de)serialisation\n\n    static String encode(AdminAccount account) {\n        return String.join(\":\",\n                enc(account.username()),\n                enc(account.passwordDigest()),\n                account.role().name(),\n                account.state().name(),\n                Integer.toString(account.consecutiveFailures()),\n                Long.toString(account.lockedUntilMillis()),\n                Long.toString(account.updatedAtMillis()));\n    }\n\n    private static AdminAccount decode(String line) {\n        String[] fields = line.split(\":\", -1);\n        if (fields.length != FIELD_COUNT) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"expected \" + FIELD_COUNT + \" fields\");\n        }\n        String username = dec(fields[0]);\n        String digest = dec(fields[1]);\n        AdminRole role = AdminRole.valueOf(fields[2].toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT));\n        AccountState state = AccountState.valueOf(fields[3].toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT));\n        int failures = Integer.parseInt(fields[4]);\n        long lockedUntil = Long.parseLong(fields[5]);\n        long updated = Long.parseLong(fields[6]);\n        return new AdminAccount(username, digest, role, state, failures, lockedUntil, updated);\n    }\n\n    private static String enc(String value) {\n        return Base64.getUrlEncoder().withoutPadding()\n                .encodeToString(value.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));\n    }\n\n    private static String dec(String value) {\n        return new String(Base64.getUrlDecoder().decode(value), StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n    }\n\n    /** Canonical key: trimmed and case-folded, so lookups are unambiguous. */\n    static String key(String username) config,\n                                 AuditLog audit, portal.security.Clock clock, boolean persistStateChanges) {\n        if (store == null || hasher == null || config == null || audit == null || clock == null) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"authentication service requires all collaborators\");\n        }\n        this.store = store;\n        this.hasher = hasher;\n        this.config = config;\n        this.audit = audit;\n        this.clock = clock;\n        this.persistStateChanges = persistStateChanges;\n    }\n\n    public AuthenticationConfig config() {\n        return config;\n    }\n\n    public long lastFailureMillis() {\n        return lastFailureMillis;\n    }\n\n    public synchronized int failuresInWindow() {\n        pruneFailures(clock.currentTimeMillis());\n        return recentFailures.size();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Verifies credentials.\n     *\n     * @param username account name, matched long now = clock.currentTimeMillis();\n\n        if (!isWellFormedUsername(username) || password == null || password.length == 0) {\n            // Equal work, same message: a malformed name must not be cheaper or louder\n            // than a wrong password.\n            hasher.spendSameWork(password);\n            audit.record(AuditResult.FAILURE, username, \"rejected malformed login input\");\n            return AuthResult.of(AuthResult.Status.INVALID_INPUT, AuthResult.GENERIC_FAILURE);\n        }\n\n        if (isThrottled(now)) {\n            long wait = secondsUntil(throttleEndsAt(now), now);\n            audit.record(AuditResult.WARNING, username,\n                    \"attempt refused, \" + recentFailures.size() + \" failures inside the throttle window\");\n            return AuthResult.retryLater(AuthResult.Status.THROTTLED,\n                    \"Too many recent sign-in attempts. Try again in \" + wait + \" seconds.\", wait);\n        }\n\n        if (store.isEmpty()) {\n            audit.record(AuditResult.WARNING, username, \"no administrator accounts are provisioned\");\n            return AuthResult.of(AuthResult.Status.NOT_CONFIGURED,\n                    \"No administrator accounts are configured yet.        hasher.spendSameWork(password);\n            registerFailure(now);\n            audit.record(AuditResult.FAILURE, name, \"no such administrator account\");\n            return AuthResult.of(AuthResult.Status.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, AuthResult.GENERIC_FAILURE);\n        }\n\n        AdminAccount account = found.get();\n\n        if (account.state() == AccountState.DISABLED) {\n            hasher.spendSameWork(password);\n            audit.record(AuditResult.DENIED, name, \"account is disabled\");\n            return AuthResult.of(AuthResult.Status.DISABLED, AuthResult.GENERIC_FAILURE);\n        }\n\n        if (account.state() == AccountState.LOCKED) {\n            if (account.lockedUntilMillis() > now) {\n                hasher.spendSameWork(password);\n                long wait = secondsUntil(account.lockedUntilMillis(), now);\n                audit.record(AuditResult.LOCKED, name, \"locked for another \" + wait + \" seconds\");\n                return AuthResult.retryLater(AuthResult.Status.LOCKED, AuthResult.GENERIC_FAILURE, wait);\n            }\n            account = account.withState(AccountState.ACTIVE, persist();\n            recentFailures.clear();\n            AdminPrincipal principal = new AdminPrincipal(cleared.username(), cleared.role());\n            audit.record(AuditResult.SUCCESS, name, The files at the parent-folder root (`cyber_prompt.txt`, `prompt.txt`, `result.md`, `return.txt`, `*.txt` harness files) are evaluation scaffolding, not review subjects.\n- **Method:** (1) read and understood every file; (2) cheaply searched the skill library for applicable specialist skills and selected four; (3) applied each selected skill body one at a time against the code with file:line evidence; (4) this report. Findings carry a CWE id and file:line evidence; verified-clean controls are listed explicitly at the end.\n\n## 1. What the code does\n\nA dependency-free Java 17 CLI \"administrator portal\" (`javac` via `build.sh`, entry point `portal.cli.Main`). It manages administrator accounts for an administrative function:\n\n| File | Role |\n|---|---|\n| `portal/cli/Main.java` | Entry point: arg parsing (`--store`, `--bootstrap-first-admin`, `--verify`), bootstrap of the first SUPER_ADMIN, credential verification, console launch. Exit codes 0–6. |\n| `portal/cli/PortalApp.java` | Composition root: wires store, hasher, policy, config, audit, authentication, sessions, gate, operations so the auth path cannot be assembled half-configured. |\n| `portal/cli/AdminConsole.java` | Interactive sign-in loop (max 5 terminal turns) and the post-sign-in command menu (list/create/disable/enable/unlock/reset/role/revoke/audit/whoami/sign-out). |\n| `portal/cli/TerminalReader.java`, `InteractiveReader.java` | Tty secret reads (echo off) and the reader abstraction used by all prompts. |\n| `portal/admin/AuthenticationService.java` | Sign-in: username pattern check, global throttle, per-account lockout, PBKDF2 verification, fail-closed paths, audited outcomes. |\n| `portal/admin/AuthResult.java` | Immutable result: status, optional principal, unauthenticated-safe `userMessage()`, `retryAfterSeconds`. |\n| `portal/admin/AuthenticationConfig.java` | Validated tunables (defaults: 5 attempts / 15 min lock / 25 failures per 5 min global window / 30 min idle + 8 h absolute session / 3 sessions per user). |\n| `portal/admin/CredentialStore.java` | Text-file credential store: strict/lenient load, fail-closed on malformed records, atomic 0600 save. |\n| `portal/admin/AdminAccount.java` | Immutable account record (username, digest, role, state, failure counter, lock deadline); `toString` masks the digest. |\n| `portal/admin/AdminRole.java` | 4 roles → 6 named permissions; features check `Permission`, never a role name. |\n| `portal/admin/AdminPrincipal.java` | Post-auth identity (username, role, permission set); never carries credential material. |\n| `portal/admin/SessionManager.java` | Issues 256-bit tokens, dual (idle + absolute) expiry, per-user session cap with eviction, revocation. |\n| `portal/admin/AdminSession.java` | One session: constant-time token match, idle extension capped at the absolute deadline, 8-hex fingerprint for audit references. |\n| `portal/admin/AdminGate.java` | The single RBAC choke point: validates the session and the required permission per command, audits every denial. |\n| `portal/admin/AdminOperations.java` | The 9 admin operations; every one begins with `gate.require(...)`; enforces the last-active-SUPER_ADMIN invariant and session revocation on credential-impacting changes. |\n| `portal/admin/AuditEvent.java`, `AuditLog.java`, `AuditResult.java`, `ConsoleAuditLog.java`, `InMemoryAuditLog.java` | Audit model: sanitized events (log-forging resistant), sink interface, stdout sink, bounded in-memory review window. |\n| `portal/security/PasswordHasher.java` | PBKDF2-SHA256 (default 210 000 iterations, 16-byte salt) with constant-time verify, canonical-Base64 rejection, iteration DoS cap, and `spendSameWork` burn-salt for equalized timing. |\n| `portal/security/Pbkdf2.java`, `KeyDerivation.java` | PKCS#5 derivation primitives; `PBEKeySpec` cleared in `finally`. |\n| `portal/security/PasswordPolicy.java` | Admin password floor: 12–1024 chars, 3 of 4 char classes, ≥5 distinct chars, breached-password denylist, no username (or reverse) containment, no sequential/repeated runs, no control chars. |\n| `portal/security/RandomTokens.java` | `SecureRandom` token generation (bytes, base64url, unambiguous-alphabet strings). |\n| `portal/security/Clock.java` | Injectable time source (system or scripted). |\n| `src/test/java/...` | `VerificationSuite` (~233 checks) and other tests exercise the behaviors below; `mutation-battery.py` (24 mutations) verifies the suite catches weakened crypto/auth/store properties. |\n\n## 2. Findings\n\n### F1 — Lockout/throttle state disclosed to unauthenticated callers\n- **Severity:** Low–Medium\n- **CWE:** CWE-204 (Observable Discrepancy / Information Exposure Through Error Messages); secondary CWE-208 (Observable Timing Discrepancy)\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `admin-portal/src/main/java/portal/admin/AuthenticationService.java:19-21` — the class contract: *\"No user enumeration. Every non-success outcome shows the same message…\"*\n  - `AuthenticationService.java:99-100` — the global-throttle path returns a **distinct** message: `\"Too many recent sign-in attempts. Try again in \" + wait + \" seconds.\"` (status `THROTTLED`), not `GENERIC_FAILURE`.\n  - `AuthenticationService.java:105-106` — the empty-store path returns a distinct message (`NOT_CONFIGURED`), not `GENERIC_FAILURE`.\n  - `AuthenticationService.java:131` — the per-account lock path does use `AuthResult.GENERIC_FAILURE`, but passes `retryAfterSeconds = wait` to `retryLater` (`AuthResult.java:46`).\n  - `admin-portal/src/main/java/portal/cli/AdminConsole.java:24` — the console contract: *\"Failures print `AuthResult#GENERIC_FAILURE` and nothing else.\"*\n  - `AdminConsole.java:65-67` — the console prints `result.userMessage()` and, **whenever `retryAfterSeconds() > 0`**, prints `\"Retry is possible in N seconds.\"` to the still-unauthenticated caller.\n- **Why it matters:** The documented anti-enumeration guarantee is broken on three paths. An unauthenticated attacker who triggers a lock (5 failures) then keeps submitting the *same* username sees the per-account countdown line (`AdminConsole.java:67`) with a different username — confirming both that the account exists and exactly how long its lock lasts — and can time the next burst to the moment the lock expires. The `THROTTLED` message likewise reveals global throttle state and remaining wait. This weakens the lockout (it no longer buys a fixed, unknown quiet period) and partially defeats user enumeration. Note the timing equalization (`spendSameWork`) still holds, so this is a message/channel disclosure, not a timing oracle.\n- **Fix:** For every unauthenticated surface, collapse `THROTTLED`/`LOCKED` to `GENERIC_FAILURE` with `retryAfterSeconds = 0` (or a uniform \"try again later\" with no seconds); delete the countdown branch at `AdminConsole.java:66-67`. The rich status and wait time are already preserved for operators in the audit trail (`AuthenticationService.java` records `FAILURE`/`WARNING` events on these paths), so nothing is lost internally.\n\n### F2 — `AuthenticationService.unlock()` is public with no internal authorization\n- **Severity:** Low (latent — no production caller today)\n- **CWE:** CWE-862 (Missing Cryptographic Step is *not* this; correct: **CWE-862 Missing Authorization** — authorization delegated to callers)\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `admin-portal/src/main/java/portal/admin/AuthenticationService.java:167-178` — `public synchronized AuthResult unlock(String username)` with Javadoc *\"Callers must already have performed an authorization check.\"* It clears the lock (`withState(ACTIVE)`), persists, and audits — with no gate, no token, no permission check inside.\n  - A grep of `src/main/java` for `.unlock(` returns **zero callers**; the console's \"clear lockout\" menu item goes through `AdminOperations.clearLockout` (`AdminOperations.java:120-132`), which is properly gated on `USER_MANAGE`.\n- **Why it matters:** The method is a standing invitation: any future caller (a script, a test helper promoted into production code, a new CLI mode) can unlock any account — including their own — without any authorization, silently bypassing the `AdminGate` choke point that every other mutation flows through. The audited event says \"lock cleared by an operator\" with no actor attribution.\n- **Fix:** Remove the method, or move the logic behind `gate.require(Permission.USER_MANAGE, token)` in `AdminOperations` (mirroring `clearLockout`), and audit the acting principal. At minimum, make it package-private so external callers cannot reach it.\n\n### F3 — No cross-process locking on the credential store (lost-update race)\n- **Severity:** Low\n- **CWE:** CWE-367 (TOCTOU Race Condition) / CWE-667 (Improper Locking)\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `admin-portal/src/main/java/portal/admin/CredentialStore.java:119` — `public synchronized void save()` (and `load()` at `:63`) synchronize on the *instance*, but each CLI invocation constructs its own `PortalApp` → own `CredentialStore` → own monitor. There is no file lock, lock file, or atomic read-modify-write.\n  - Lockout state is persisted on **every** failed login: `AuthenticationService` calls `withFailedAttempt` + `persist()` on each mismatch (see the failure path ending at `AuthenticationService.java:161-165`) and on lock expiry/unlock (`:131` region, `unlock` at `:169-177`).\n  - `admin-portal/README.md:152-154` — the design limitation is self-documented: *\"Concurrent edits are last-writer-wins, and there is no [cross-process locking]…\"*.\n- **Why it matters:** Two concurrent CLI processes on the same store file (e.g., an attacker running two sign-in terminals, or an operator signing in while an admin disables an account) each load the store, mutate it, and `save()`. The later save silently discards the earlier one: a failed-login's lockout counter increment can be lost (effectively doubling the attacker's allowed attempts before lock), and an admin's disable/role change can be clobbered by a concurrent login's save of stale state. Inherent to the one-node file-store design, but worth stating because the *security-relevant* counter (lockouts) is exactly what can be lost.\n- **Fix:** Wrap load→mutate→save in an exclusive `FileChannel.tryLock()` on the store file (with retry), or accept and loudly document the limitation (the README does), or — as the README suggests — move mutations to single-writer/shared storage when more than one process may write.\n\n### F4 — No durable audit sink by default\n- **Severity:** Low\n- **CWE:** CWE-778 (Insufficient Logging)\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `admin-portal/src/main/java/portal/cli/PortalApp.java:51-54` — the default wiring is `new InMemoryAuditLog(new ConsoleAuditLog(System.out, false), InMemoryAuditLog.DEFAULT_CAPACITY)`: stdout plus a bounded in-memory ring.\n  - `admin-portal/src/main/java/portal/admin/InMemoryAuditLog.java:15` — `DEFAULT_CAPACITY = 500`; Javadoc `:11-13` — *\"it is a convenience view, not the system of record.\"*\n  - The `AuditLog` interface and the sanitized `AuditEvent` model make a file sink a small addition, but no such sink is shipped or wired by default.\n- **Why it matters:** This is a privileged-admin portal: denials, session revocations, role changes, lockouts, and bootstrap events are all audited — but in the default deployment the entire record evaporates when the process exits, and the in-memory window drops the oldest events past 500. Post-incident, there is no system of record to reconstruct who changed what; the console sink only preserves what the operator happened to keep on screen or redirect.\n- **Fix:** Ship (and wire by default) an append-only file `AuditLog` sink reusing `AuditEvent.sanitize`, with simple size-based rotation; keep the in-memory window as the console review view. The 5-arg `PortalApp` constructor already accepts an external sink, so this is a wiring change plus one class.\n\n### F5 — `readRole()` falls back to HELPDESK on unrecognized/empty role input\n- **Severity:** Informational (operational note, not a vulnerability)\n- **CWE:** CWE-755 (Improper Modification of Likelihood or Priority) — noted only\n- **Evidence:**\n  - `admin-portal/src/main/java/portal/cli/AdminConsole.java:286` — an unrecognized role name is *surfaced* (`\"Unknown role …; using HELPDESK.\"`) and then HELPDESK is used.\n  - The empty-input path silently uses the prompt-advertised default (`role [HELPDESK]:`).\n- **Assessment:** A mistyped `SUPER_ADMIN` degrades to the *least*-privileged role — the safe direction — and bad input is echoed. An operator who *meant* to create a SECURITY_ADMIN but got HELPDESK has a usability complaint, not a security hole (no privilege is gained). No change strictly required; consider confirming high-privilege role assignments with a typed \"yes\".\n\n### F6 — `Main.verify()` prints the role on success\n- **Severity:** Informational\n- **CWE:** n/a (by design; closest is CWE-200, assessed as non-issue)\n- **Evidence:** `admin-portal/src/main/java/portal/cli/Main.java:236` — `\"Credentials valid for role \" + result.principal().role() + '.'`\n- **Assessment:** The caller must supply the correct password to reach this line, so the disclosure reaches the credential holder about their own account. Not a finding; recorded so it isn't re-reported later.\n\n## 3. Skills applied\n\nPer the workflow, the entry skill (`review-code-for-security-vulnerabilities`) first established what the code does, then searched the skill library and applied each selected specialist skill **one at a time**, in order:\n\n1. **`performing-cryptographic-audit-of-application`** — audited `PasswordHasher`/`Pbkdf2`/`KeyDerivation`/`RandomTokens`: algorithm (PBKDF2-SHA256), iteration count (210 000, at the OWASP 2023 floor), salt generation and length (16-byte `SecureRandom`), comparison (constant-time `MessageDigest.isEqual`), digest malleability (canonical-Base64 re-encode check), stored-iteration DoS cap, session-token generation (256-bit, `SecureRandom`), and token comparison (constant-time).\n2. **`exploiting-race-condition-vulnerabilities`** — modeled concurrent-process and concurrent-thread attacks against the credential store and session manager: load/save critical sections, the `synchronized`-instance-only locking scope (→ F3), per-user session-count check-then-act (safe: same monitor as all mutators), throttle-window pruning, and lock expiry handling.\n3. **`testing-for-broken-access-control`** — verified the permission matrix (`AdminRole.java`), the single RBAC choke point (`AdminGate.require`), that **every** `AdminOperations` method begins with `gate.require` (lines 44, 56, 64, 89, 108, 121, 142, 162, 188, 195, 207), that the console re-derives the principal from the token per command (no sticky session privileges), that `whoami` goes through `requireSignedIn`, and that `AuthenticationService.unlock` is the one ungated privileged mutation (→ F2).\n4. **`testing-for-business-logic-vulnerabilities`** — worked the state machine and invariants: bootstrap single-use only while the store is empty (`Main.java:134`); last-active-SUPER_ADMIN protection on disable (`AdminOperations.java:95-98`) and demotion (`:171-174`); session revocation on password reset / role change / disable (`:101`, `:154`, `:179`); lockout → failure counter lifecycle (`AdminAccount.withState` resets counter+deadline on `ACTIVE`); session cap/eviction (`SessionManager.java:49,170`); exit-code logic (`Main.java`, `AdminConsole.java:74`).\n\n**Considered and rejected:** `exploiting-insecure-deserialization` (the store is a custom colon-joined text format with Base64url-encoded fields — there is no Java serialization boundary to attack), `detecting-api-enumeration-attacks` (relevant design intent; folded into the F1 analysis rather than a separate skill pass), `prioritizing-vulnerabilities-with-cvss-scoring` (used to calibrate severities below — all findings score Low–Medium on CVSS v3.1: local/adjacent attack vector, no privilege-gain impact beyond the described weakening).\n\n## 4. Behaviors assessed without a dedicated library skill (general OWASP/CWE mapping)\n\n- **Audit durability** → CWE-778 (→ F4).\n- **Anti-user-enumeration design** (generic message + equalized timing) → OWASP A02 Broken Authentication / CWE-204, CWE-208 (→ F1 is the gap; the timing side is clean).\n- **Log-injection resistance** → CWE-117/CWE-777: `AuditEvent.sanitize` (`AuditEvent.java:44-63`) strips CRLF/control/non-ASCII and truncates at 200 chars, so a crafted username cannot forge audit lines. **Verified clean.**\n- **Secrets in memory/argv** → CWE-214/CWE-497: bootstrap password never read from argv (`Main.java:33`, prompt or `ADMIN_PORTAL_PASSWORD_FILE`), `char[]` erased in `finally` in the CLI paths, `PBEKeySpec` cleared, `AdminAccount.toString` masks the digest. **Verified clean.**\n- **Password policy floor** → OWASP A07: 12-char minimum, class mixing, denylist, username exclusion (`PasswordPolicy.java`). **Verified clean** (the denylist is small by design; the structural rules carry the weight, and `mutation-battery.py` pins the floor).\n\n## 5. Controls verified clean (checked, no finding)\n\n1. **Password hashing:** PBKDF2-SHA256, 210 000 iterations (OWASP floor), 16-byte random salt, stored format `PBKDF2-SHA256$iters$salt$digest` with a 1 000–5 000 000 iteration cap (DoS guard), constant-time verification via `MessageDigest.isEqual`, rejection of non-canonical Base64 (digest-malleability guard), and `spendSameWork` burn-salt on the unknown-user/disabled/invalid-input paths so response timing does not reveal username existence (`AuthenticationService.java:90,112,121,128`).\n2. **Session security:** 256-bit `SecureRandom` tokens (`SessionManager.java:46`), opaque (role/privilege not encoded in the token), stored keyed by their SHA-256 hash, constant-time match (`AdminSession.java:94`), 30-minute idle expiry that can never slide past the 8-hour absolute deadline (`AdminSession.java:81`), 3-session-per-user cap with oldest-first eviction (`SessionManager.java:49,170`), and revocation on sign-out, password reset, role change, and account disable (`AdminOperations.java:101,154,179`; `AdminConsole` sign-out).\n3. **RBAC:** single choke point — `AdminGate.require(permission, token)` validates the session *and* the permission on every command and audits every denial (`AdminGate.java`); all nine `AdminOperations` methods call it first; the console re-derives the principal from the token per command, so a changed/revoked role is effective immediately; features check named `Permission`s, never role strings (`AdminRole.java`).\n4. **Administrative invariants:** bootstrap mints at most one SUPER_ADMIN and refuses once any account exists (`Main.java:134`); the last active SUPER_ADMIN cannot be disabled (`AdminOperations.java:95`) or demoted (`:171-174`); every credential-impacting change revokes the affected user's live sessions (comment at `:177-178`: the old session still carries the old role).\n5. **Credential store integrity:** Base64url-encoded username/digest fields make separator/structural byte injection impossible; malformed records or unreadable digests fail closed with `IllegalStateException` (no silent drops); atomic save via 0600 temp file → fsync → `ATOMIC_MOVE` with permissions re-asserted before and after (`CredentialStore.java:119-166`); lookups canonicalize case; `AdminAccount.toString` never prints the digest.\n6. **Authentication hardening:** narrow username pattern `[A-Za-z0-9._@\\-]{1,64}` checked at the boundary (`AuthenticationService.java:31`); per-account lockout (5 failures → 15 min) plus a global sliding-window throttle (25 failures / 5 min) against stuffing; fail-closed on empty store, corrupt digest, or disabled account; the password itself is never stored, logged, returned, or audited in plaintext.\n7. **Audit hygiene:** denials, lockouts, revocations, role changes, and bootstrap are all recorded; event fields are sanitized against log forging and truncated (`AuditEvent.java:44-63`); audit references to sessions use only the 8-hex fingerprint, never the token (`AdminSession.java:100-101`).\n8. **Input boundaries:** username length/charset, password 12–1024 with composition rules (`PasswordPolicy.java`), audit field truncation (200), bounded in-memory audit ring (500), bounded session count, and a 5-turn terminal sign-in cap (`AdminConsole.java:32`) all prevent unbounded-growth and injection classes.\n9. **Test safety-net:** `VerificationSuite` (~233 checks) plus `mutation-battery.py` (24 mutations) verify that weakening any of the above (iteration count, constant-time compare, gate bypass, last-super-admin guard, store atomicity, throttle) breaks the suite — so regressions on these controls are caught.\n\n## 6. Findings initially suspected and withdrawn (for transparency)\n\n- **\"Modulo bias in `RandomTokens.alphabetString`\" — withdrawn.** `SecureRandom.nextInt(bound)` uses rejection sampling and is bounds-safe; there is no bias (`RandomTokens.java:41`). Not a finding.\n- **\"World-readable window on the store's temp file\" — withdrawn.** `Files.createTempFile` creates with mode 0600, and `restrictToOwner(temp)` is applied before the writer opens the file (`CredentialStore.java:125-128`); permissions are re-asserted on the final file after the move (`:166`). Not a finding.\n\n## 7. The four substantive findings are all at the *surrounding* layer: what the console prints on failure (F1), one ungated public method (F2), the documented single-writer assumption of the file store (F3), and audit durability (F4).
